# Refutation Ledger — PR #3 review loop

Purpose: single source of truth for review candidates that were **refuted or consciously accepted** during this branch's review loops (Codex R1–R21, claude-glm rounds GLM1–GLM37, property-harness rounds proph-1..3, the floor82 round, and the M3/Task-3.1 rounds below). Re-open an entry ONLY with new evidence (empirical repro, vendor-doc proof, or a demonstrated production path) — not by re-deriving the same hypothetical. Reviewers: read before flagging.

THE ROUND'S SHAPE: every finding a ROUND-62 FOLLOW-ON — THE MID-LINE ANCHOR STRAGGLER (the r62 [ \t]*-under-/m widening still statement-INITIAL only, three more legal spellings invisible while build.php's rewriter handled them), THE INLINE-HTML BRACE ARM (the trait fence the r62 widening itself forced counting T_INLINE_HTML's '{'/'}' as code braces — plus the '<?xml' shape the '?' branch treated as an open tag), and THE BUILDER-SIDE STREAM-SEPARATOR ARM (the r62 inspector fence refusing at extraction what the collector staged without complaint — the one-verdict drift the near-source pair closed one vocabulary over, now closed for ':' too).

THE MID-LINE STRAGGLER. ocr63-1 (bug:medium): the r62 anchors ('[ \t]*' under /m) still saw only statement-INITIAL lines, so three LEGAL mid-line spellings were invisible to the unused-import gate — the one-line braced namespace block ('namespace X { use A\B; }'), the second statement on a two-statement line ('use A\B; use C\D;'), and the use riding the open tag's line ('<?php use A\B;') — while bin/build.php's rewriter, whose statement-start judgment is a LOOKBEHIND with no line anchor, handled them fine: the gate drift the round-62 census claimed closed. The statement-start BOUNDARY replaces the line anchor: '(?<![label byte])' over the ONE WP_CONNECTORS_LABEL_BYTES class (the r49/r60 census's own spelling — a use token is a use STATEMENT exactly when no label byte runs into it, so mid-line after '{', ';', '}', or the open tag all satisfy it while a 'use' inside a longer label ('reuse') or riding a label's tail ('<?phpuse' — not an open tag at all, the engine's own spelling rule) never does; /m rides off with the '^'). The swap rides all three statement patterns (plain, group, comma-list) and the two keyword-prefix strips drop their dead '[ \t]*' (the matched text now BEGINS at 'use' — the r46-9 rule: the derivation strips what the widened patterns match). THE FENCE CARRIES THE WHOLE JUDGMENT now (the r62 load-bearing piece, one generation further): a one-line 'class C { use SomeTrait; }' is matched AND fenced, never anchor-blinded. DRIVEN: the dead mid-line spellings flag (RED at HEAD: 0 — the one-line namespace block, the second-on-line, the open-tag line, and the mid-line group member), the used twins and the one-line trait/comma/adaptation rows stay 0 by judgment.

THE INLINE-HTML BRACE ARM. ocr63-2 (bug:low): T_INLINE_HTML passes wp_connectors_mask_string_contents() verbatim while the r62 fence's brace walk counted its '{'/'}' as CODE braces — a legal php -l-clean plugin file whose '?>' HTML carries a template placeholder ('{placeholder}', inline JS/CSS braces) armed an 'other' frame past the reopen and every use after the HTML was judged a trait clause and skipped, dead imports escaping (RED at HEAD: 0); a stray HTML '}' popped a frame the code still owed — VERDICT-NEUTRAL on every lint-clean shape by construction (a close tag inside a class body is a PARSE ERROR, verified on this engine: the frame a trait clause needs is always pushed after the HTML), so the stray-'}' row pins the code braces judged correctly through it, not a flip; and the '?' branch treated ANY '<?' as an open tag, so a leading '<?xml … ?>' HTML head rode the same arm. The walk owns the inline-HTML arm (the file's own brace-counting doctrine for non-code regions): the engine starts in HTML mode (leading HTML included), braces and ';' in HTML never touch the frame stack, only the INI-INDEPENDENT spellings ('<?php', '<?=') open PHP mode — never a bare '<?' ('<?xml' stays HTML under the production-default short_open_tag=Off; the walk must not inherit the host's short_open_tag the masked view's own tokenizer does — THIS dev host runs it On) — and a match offset landing in HTML returns FALSE, never an import: the region judgment the r63 boundary anchor left for the fence to own (the anchor matches a column-0 or mid-line HTML 'use …;' exactly as it does a code one — the in-HTML row RED at HEAD in the false-positive direction: 1). DRIVEN: the unbalanced-brace row, the xml-led-brace row, and the in-HTML-use row red at HEAD (0, 0, 1), all correct now; the balanced-brace, clean-xml, stray-'}', and used-import controls green before and after (stash-verified for the reds).

THE BUILDER-SIDE STREAM SEPARATOR. ocr63-3 (bug:medium): the builder/inspector ONE-VERDICT violation the r62 fence itself opened — ':' is LEGAL in a POSIX filename, so collectFiles() staged 'assets/icon:2x.png' without complaint, published the zip at exit 0, and the inspector's stream fence refused the SAME entry over the SAME bytes (the t31-r5-10 one-verdict doctrine's exact shape, the near-source pair one vocabulary over; RED at HEAD: the build RETURNED a zip path). The builder owns the class at COLLECTION: a ':'-bearing segment answers the build's own loud refusal AFTER the exclusion filter (what never ships never judges the build) and BEFORE any staging or zip write — ONE class, TWO owners, ONE verdict, the inspector fence kept as DEFENSE IN DEPTH over the archive-controlled names the walk never sees. NO drive-letter exemption on the builder side (the inspector's own fence exempts the '\A[A-Za-z]:' head as ABSOLUTE-path grammar, the r39-2 vocabulary): these segments are RELATIVES under the plugin root, and the entry a drive-letter-NAMED tree node ('C:') would compose is '<slug>/C:/…' — slug-prefixed, so the inspector's head exemption never applies to it, the un-exempted per-segment refusal keeping the pair's one verdict on the pathological spelling too. The census rides BOTH sites, each naming the other. DRIVEN: the fixture tree carrying 'assets/icon:2x.png' fails the BUILD loudly at collection naming the name (RED at HEAD: stash-verified, the build green and the inspector refusing the very zip it answered), and the colon-free twin of the same tree builds and inspects green through the real buildPlugin + wp_connectors_inspect_artifact pair.

The new owner wp_connectors_use_statement_in_import_position() is build.php's brace-kind doctrine over the MASKED view: a '{' opens a NAMESPACE block exactly when the declaration run before it is the 'namespace' keyword's own (the mode tags their own run boundaries — '<?php namespace X {' is the canonical file head, the open tag's '<' would otherwise classify the brace 'other'), every other brace is 'other', and the statement is an import when no 'other' frame encloses it — every brace the walk counts being code by the mask's construction (interpolation braces masked, the t31-ocr16-10 class structurally impossible here; inline HTML the glm17-8 boundary the patterns themselves already carry). THE NTFS STREAM SEPARATOR. ocr62-2 (security:medium): every pre-extraction fence omitted ':' — the edge-junk fold class is C0+DEL+dot and the ':' grammar screen owns only the top-level slug — so '<slug>/src/shell.php:$DATA' (and 'x.php:hidden', 'x.php:anything') passed them all: the raw lens saw a non-'.php' tail, the edge-junk rtrim never strips ':', the duplicate fold keyed it apart from the plain 'shell.php' entry, the traversal fold is dots-only. On a Windows/NTFS extraction target that name lands the bytes in an ALTERNATE DATA STREAM of the colon-free file — for ':$DATA' the MAIN stream of shell.php — plugin-reachable content no content gate judged under the entry's own spelling. ONE arm owns the class at the NAME, upstream of all four seams (the r20 lesson: never extract what the host would resolve differently than the gates judged): ':' in any NON-DRIVE-LETTER position of a relative entry name answers the loud pre-extraction refusal, keyed per name the t31-ocr32-5 doctrine, the verdict line through the printable seam; the drive-letter spelling is ABSOLUTE-path grammar (the r39-2 bare-drive fence's vocabulary in the rrmdir owner, the slug grammar screen for the relative zip), its verdict unchanged, both named in the census so the exemption never reads as an oversight. ADJACENT, RECORDED: three printable-seam pins whose forged payloads incidentally spelled 'inspect:' in ENTRY names now spell 'inspect;' — the ':' is the stream fence's own refusal cause and would pre-empt the pinned verdicts (exactly the fence working); the newline/ANSI bytes those pins judge ride unchanged, the seam-level probes and the content-borne payloads keeping their ':' verbatim.

THE NBSP DUPLICATE — REFUTED, NO COMMIT. ocr62-3 (test:low): the finding read the hostile-names battery as carrying ' Authorization' twice — single-quoted and double-quoted, byte-identical — with the NBSP spelling the t31-r1-16 finding names never driven. BYTE-EXACT INSPECTION REFUTES IT: the double-quoted row is "\xC2\xA0Authorization" — the genuine U+00A0 bytes (od: c2a0 41757468…), a byte sequence that RENDERS as a leading space in every text tool, which is precisely how the finding (and the executing implementer's first read) was fooled. There is no duplicate: the battery carries trailing-space, leading-space (0x20), semicolon, NBSP (c2a0), split-word, Cyrillic-г, fullwidth-ｒ, CRLF-injection, NUL, and TAB spellings, all distinct at the byte level; the docblock's claim ('the leading-space, semicolon, NBSP, and homoglyph siblings') is TRUE and the NBSP sibling IS driven — NAME_TOKEN_PATTERN rejecting it by construction every run. No change made; re-open only with a byte-level demonstration of an actual duplicate.

THE CHILD-OWNERSHIP FINALLY. ocr62-4 (test:low): the crash-sim reap pin's LIVE direction spawned its sleep(30) child OUTSIDE any try/finally — both assertions between the spawn and killChildIfAlive() could abort the leg and orphan the sleeping child for up to its full 30 seconds, the exact never-left-looping contract the assertion's own message names the finally keeping. The leg wraps its child in the file's own idiom (the freeze-loop leg's frame, one test over): spawn inside try, killChildIfAlive in the finally behind the $livePid > 0 guard (the guard also holding the staging-failure shape). CONSTRUCTION-EVIDENT: the leg's verdicts and bounded waits byte-identical, only the ownership frame added.

THE getLastErrors NARRATIVE — PREMISE REFUTED, RESIDUAL FIXED. ocr62-5 (documentation:low ×2): the finding claimed both comments record the history INVERTED — that pre-8.3 handed FALSE on a clean parse and 8.3+ ALWAYS hands an array, so 'an 8.3+ runner CANNOT hand false after a createFromFormat() parse'. Instead, it would always return the documented array structure.'). Round 43's t31-ocr43-9 direction is VINDICATED (array-before/false-after); the r62 inversion claim would have re-introduced round 26's backwards story. The one TRUE residual — the VERSION ANCHOR (the rewrite landed in 8.2.0, not 8.3) — is corrected in both sites, the probe and the changelog entry recorded as evidence; the guard is and stays shape-agnostic (false and the zero-count array both the clean verdict), no runtime branch added. THE 'signature' SUFFIX ARM. ocr61-2 (security:medium): the suffix class carried no 'signature' arm — Stripe's 'Stripe-Signature', GitHub's 'X-Hub-Signature', the generic 'X-Signature', and Google's 'x-goog-signature' fold to a final token 'signature' that matched neither catalog nor suffix, so is_sensitive_header_name('Stripe-Signature') answered false and HeaderMap's masked render showed the credential-derived HMAC material verbatim in every safe debug form (driven at HEAD) — the r12-4/ocr15-1 leak class under spellings the vendors document themselves; verified NOT an adjudicated exclusion (the battery pins the families, the deliberate 'www-auth' exclusion is elsewhere). 'signature' joins — one member speaking every delimiter spelling per the r50-1 boundary doctrine (the bare token, every segment tail, '_'/'.' spellings); the 'signature-key' shapes need no member of their own (every '…-signature-key' ends '-key', the generic tier owns them); no flattened glued twin joins (every motivating header hyphenates, the r55-1 'authentication' treatment); and the '-256' VARIANT rides its own two-token entry 'signature-256' — the fold leaves the variant's judged final segment '256', a token no credential name spells, so the tail WITH the variant is judged whole (the same final-segment boundary, one entry longer; probed: 'X-Hub-Signature-256' masked, 'X-Signature256' still outside). THE RESERVED-SEGMENT GENERATION. The round's own lesson is that the ENGINE'S boundary is not one line but a MAP, and each position owns a different slice of it — every verdict below php -l-derived on this runner's 8.5.10 before a line of the fix was written. ocr47-1 (bug:medium): the member-NAME check (ocr43-3) validated label SHAPE only, and every keyword of the language is legal label bytes — `use Deicod\WpConnectors\{Shared\Clock, list};` matched the vendor-prefix pattern, the bare `list` survived the shape verdict, and both re-emit seams (the vendor-prefix callback and the family-prefix brace tail) re-emitted it verbatim beside the rewritten sibling: parse-error bytes at exit 0 with every gate green (DRIVEN at HEAD: the ship shape returned normally; the alone rows refused one verdict late at the postcondition's survived-reference walk). Two driven halves now: the BARE-KEYWORD half — a member whose whole spelling is one keyword token (list/foreach/static/LIST/function list) — rides the ONE identifier walk EXTRACTED from the alias oracle (a single-segment member occupies the same one-identifier slot; the lexer tokens a bare identifier identically whatever frames it); the SPECIAL-CLASS-LEAF half — a member whose last segment is one of the FIFTEEN special class names (the alias oracle's soft hand list folded into ONE const, plus 'static' whose alias verdict rides unchanged through its walk) — an un-aliased class import binding one FATALS at compile ("Cannot use A\B\true as true because 'true' is a special class name"), mid-name or bare. THE DISSOLVERS are position premises the grammar owns and refuses to refuse: an alias binds X ('{Storage\true as X}' lints clean), a function/const kind exempts the leaf ('{function true}' lints clean — the fatal is a CLASS-name check), and a keyword GLUED into a multi-segment member ('{list\Bar}', '{Shared\Storage\list}') parses clean and rides — a three-spelling dissolver battery pins all three rewriting. ocr47-2 (bug:medium): the same vocabulary gap at the TAIL and LIST seams — `use …\Shared\true;` matched (the tail `\true` label-shaped per the r46 anchor), group 2 re-emitted verbatim beside the rewritten family, and the zip shipped the compile FATAL at exit 0 (DRIVEN: returned normally); the relative-use walk's member judgment (token-SHAPE only) shipped the same fatal bytes twice over — `use namespace\Clock, Shared\Storage\true;` (a T_NAME_QUALIFIED piece is a name token like any other) and the relative run's OWN leaf `use namespace\true;`. DRIVEN HONESTLY (the ocr46-8 doctrine): the finding's named spelling (`list`) lints clean at the tail — the 8.0+ lexer GLUES a hard keyword in a non-first segment into the name token (`use …\Shared\list;` parses; probed), so the tail's refused class is the special-class LEAF alone and the engine owns no bare-keyword class there at all; the commit message and census comment name the counter-proof. The tail check rides the un-aliased class-kind binding only (alias dissolves, kind dissolves, a brace tail turns the tail into a mid-name prefix the members' own grammar judges); the relative walk judges each member's leaf at its completion, kind-led from the previous code token, its own run included. THE SWEEP'S THIRD SEAM is adjudicated CLEAN by the same oracle: the namespace-declaration twin owns NO reserved vocabulary (`namespace Foo\true;` and `namespace true;` both lint clean on 8.5.10 — the fatal is a use-binding check; the r46 label anchor stays that seam's whole grammar), documented at the census const. THE MULTI-TAIL ADJUDICATION. ocr47-4 (bug:low): the probe's repeated '/..' strip loop pops NOTHING — '/a/b/../..' probes '/a/b', never the semantic '/' — and the ocr9-1 doctrine had adjudicated that landing for the SINGLE tail only, the multi-tail spelling never derived a verdict for. READ THE LEDGER FIRST, as the finding charged: the ocr9-1 entry's own derivation EXTENDS — the landing is the PROBE spelling, never a resolver, and a popped component would remove a link's own spelling from the chain the anchor walk judges ('link/x/../..' popped to '/' hides the link — the ocr9-1 blast radius, multi-tail edition), while every component of a spelling IS traversed by the engine's own resolution (the kernel walks INTO 'a/b' before either '..' cancels it, following any link there), so the no-pop landing retains exactly the components the resolution touches — the ocr17-2 full-chain reach whole. The adjudication lands as the census comment at the loop and two pins: the multi-tail spelling THROUGH a link answers the link refusal naming the link (red under the popping correction — the landing collapses to '/', the probe sees no link, the copy walks the blast radius), and a REAL multi-tail source still copies the tree it semantically names (the WALKS keep the caller's spelling — the ocr9-1 split, only the probe strips). Construction-green at HEAD by the derived doctrine; single-tail landings unchanged.

THE ORPHANED CHILD AND THE DRIVEN COUNTER-PROOF. ocr47-8+47-9 (other:medium, one commit with the census folded): the crash-sim child ran `while (true)` with no in-child termination, its only lifecycle owner the test's finally kill — a CI cancel/timeout, an OOM fatal, or a Ctrl-C skips finally and orphans a busy-looping child (reflection + stat + glob per iteration) beside a dead test. The child embeds the OWNING test process's pid (the getppid() drift is USELESS here — the spawn shell exits at once, the child re-parented at birth) and probes it every iteration through the build's own processIsAlive doctrine spelled inline — with its branches REORDERED by a driven counter-proof the fix's own first cut would have shipped: the doctrine's is_dir-first /proc branch CANNOT serve a hot loop, because PHP's stat cache pins the first verdict per path string — an entry that read alive at loop start kept answering ALIVE over TWENTY MILLION iterations (3+ seconds, climbing) past the owner's death, driven in /tmp; the sim's own first "failure" was twice misread before the reaping/zombie and cache mechanisms were separated (both recorded in the round's working notes: a zombie answers kill -0 true until reaped, and the stat cache never re-stats at all). THE AUTHORITY TWIN AND THE MASKING SLICE. ocr44-1 (security:medium): the URL Standard removes ALL ASCII tabs and newlines from the input BEFORE parsing, so a tab in the host mutates the host a browser contacts — 'https://id\tp.example/device' sends a WHATWG consumer to idp.example (DRIVEN) while this parse kept the tab in the authority verbatim and the engine's own parse_url() rewrote it to a THIRD spelling ('id_p.example', probed): one URL naming three hosts over the same browser-facing channel (the device-flow verification URI, passed through raw) the ocr28-6 backslash screen closed. ## M3 / Task 3.1 OCR round 28 (branch feat/m3-task31-contracts, 2026-09-18)

TWENTY-EIGHTH OCR-TOOL ROUND (62/62, complete): 9 findings; driver triage accepted all (t31-ocr28-1..8 — eight finding commits, the two same-class copyTree test-fence sites sharing one commit, plus the docs record, the full offline check green after every commit). TRAJECTORY 27→10→11→7→11→33→9→9→4→12→8→7→9→4→11→15→15→9. THE ROUND'S SHAPE: the TWIN PROBLEM AGAIN — the heredoc lens missed the EOF flush its name-walk sibling got one round earlier (census lesson named: a fix applies to the CLASS, and the sibling lens of the same detector IS the class), plus the trivia-separated leading separator (the interrupted-absolute spelling — token-stream shapes, not byte shapes, are the frontier now), and the round carries the loop's own engine-premise discipline: one finding's compile-time-refusal premise was DRIVEN at fix time and REFUTED on this host (the r21 doctrine — drive the engine claim before the fix lands — applied in-round, before the commit, not at a verifier pass after it).

THE DETECTOR'S TWIN EOF BOUNDARY. ocr28-1 (bug:high, the round's head): the text lens's heredoc state had no EOF flush — the flush lived inline under T_END_HEREDOC alone, so a source truncated inside the heredoc (the tokenizer answers T_START_HEREDOC then T_ENCAPSED_AND_WHITESPACE chunks and never a label) met no flush, the open state died with the loop, and every finding the body carried dropped without its report (driven red at HEAD: zero references where the terminated twin reports two; the build shipped the bytes at exit 0) — the SAME totality gap t31-ocr27-3 closed for the name walk's group prefix one round earlier, missed in the sibling lens. The flush rides ONE closure (the LENS half: per-chunk text lens, the whole-body value lens with the nowdoc split and the ocr20-3 leading-backslash tolerance, the dynamic-body K1 stand-down) while the loop keeps the STATE half and resets it at the handler — the label boundary and EOF call the same judgments, never to drift. The truncated heredoc/nowdoc/interpolated spellings each answer EXACTLY their terminated twin's verdict (assertSame over the whole reference arrays), the empty truncated body reports nothing (the flush judges, never manufactures).

THE TOKEN-STREAM FRONTIER. ocr28-2 (bug:high): the INTERRUPTED ABSOLUTE — a leading separator standing apart from its name (`\ Deicod\…`, whitespace or comment trivia between) lexes as a standalone T_NS_SEPARATOR the non-name branch consumed (PHP's lexer bakes the backslash into a name token only when nothing interrupts), and the qualified name rode on as relative: `$is_absolute_run` read false, the group prefix COMPOSED the member (`Psr\Log\Deicod\…`, the r10-9 laundering verdict — driven red at HEAD at ZERO references while the glued twin reported, the build shipping at exit 0). A standalone separator the run assembly did not swallow ARMS the absolute expectation (the interrupted-relative sibling's own pending-arm pattern, t31-ocr11-21): a run only ever starts at a name token, so a separator followed modulo trivia by a name is LEADING by construction; the arm dies at the same grammar guard the relative twin dies at, and qualifiedness includes it so the alias slot's interrupted tail reports like its glued twin. Every interruption spelling answers the glued twin's un-composed verdict; the interrupted absolute-only body trips the empty-body fence exactly as the glued body does (the ocr11-4 parity); the dangling separators and the `use Prefix \ {` brace join stay silent/composing (that separator is the prefix's own).

THE PLATFORM PREMISE, BOTH SIDES. ocr28-3 (bug:medium): the copyTree resolution machinery's cwd-prepend arm premised POSIX spelling with no platform gate at the owner — "a target not starting with '/' is relative" is FALSE on a non-POSIX host for the host's own absolute spellings ('C:\Temp\dst', a UNC root), which were cwd-prepended into a collapse that joined realpath()'s backslash output through '/' separators into a spelling no host resolves. The arm consults the ONE platform owner (isPosixHost(), the t31-ocr23-6 hoist, the probe the root-anchored battery and both TMPDIR sims already consume) plus a drive-letter/UNC root-shape check naming the premise: named refusal, never a cwd-prepend. The POSIX-side exactness is DRIVEN (the drive-letter spelling lands as a legal relative target through its true tree — the gate never over-refuses); the non-POSIX refusal is construction-evident (DIRECTORY_SEPARATOR is a platform constant no test sim flips in-process or in the child engines). ocr28-8 (test:low ×2, ONE commit) is the fence-side twin: FOUR legs asserted through POSIX-shaped machinery with no gate — the nested same-name leg (its subject IS the '/'-joined prefix strip), the trailing-slash leg (a trailing '/' names the last-component separator only where '/' is the separator), the precondition control legs (the t31-ocr11-2 root-resolution doctrine), the relative-target leg (the cwd-prepend arm's own premise, gated at the production owner this same round) — each gates visibly now, its skip naming ITS OWN premise; and the bare-'/' source-root leg's generic assertion was VACUOUS (every copyTree refusal starts 'WpHarness::copyTree() refuses', a string containing '/' — the contains-'/' check could never fail), so the leg pins the source-side root-collapse VOCABULARY instead, a substring only that refusal carries, plus the caller's bare root spelling in the tail.

THE ENGINE-PREMISE REFUTATION OF THE ROUND. ocr28-4 (bug:low): the finding claimed the engine refuses surrogate codepoints (0xD800–0xDFFF) at compile time "with the very error" the non-hex comment cites — the DRIVEN engine refutes it: on this host (PHP 8.5.10, php -l and runtime, byte-hexed) every surrogate spelling compiles CLEAN and computes its raw three-byte UTF-8 ('\u{D800}' → ED A0 80) — the RFC-era refusal the Unicode-escape RFC spelled was LIFTED upstream, and only the over-range and non-hex spellings still refuse. Making the class literal would have INVENTED a refusal the running engine does not give — the ocr23-4 defect class (the model diverging from the lexer it exists to mirror) inverted. Per the r21 doctrine (an engine-matrix premise is a vendor/record claim — drive it BEFORE the fix lands), the premise was driven at fix time, refuted, and the close is the driven adjudication: the production behavior stands, the range check carries the surrogate record at its boundary, and the pin drives the ENGINE ITSELF as the oracle (the ocr16-7 eval idiom) over every range boundary — D7FF/D800/DBFF/DFFF/E000 — assertSame between the eval'd engine value and the unescaper's answer. VERSION-DRIFT NOTE (ledgered residual below): an older engine generation that still refuses the class fails that pin loudly (the eval IS the compile-time refusal there) — the honest signal on a host the mirror does not serve, never a red to suppress.

THE SMALL GUARDS. ocr28-5 (bug:low): $line_of ignored preg_match_all()'s false, the abort riding the arithmetic as false + 1 = 1 — a line-count abort at any depth answered line 1, misattributing every text finding's quoted position; the false answers the abort guard now (line 0, the named unknowable for a 1-based field), the sibling shape the lens's own pcre-abort row carries — construction-evident, a \R count cannot be driven to abort without engine limits no spellable subject reaches. ocr28-6 (security:low, DERIVED FIRST per the finding's own instruction): the URL authority-termination set was '/?#' only and the backslash rode the authority verbatim — internally harmless on the PHP side (driven: parse_url and the rebuilt authority agree, the transport rides the same engine semantics, no consumer of this VO splits on the byte) EXCEPT at the one browser-facing channel: the device-flow verification URI passes through RAW to the authorization redirect, a WHATWG browser terminates the authority at '\', and 'https://evil.example\@idp.example/' sends the browser to evil.example while this parse, the rebuilt authority, and every redacted form name idp.example — the host-forgery seam the round-1 space/tab adjudication spared those bytes from (they render oddly but forge nothing; the backslash re-splits the authority in a consumer that renders it). RFC 3986's authority grammar carries no backslash in host, userinfo, or port, so the refusal rejects nothing legal; the screen rides the derived authority segment itself (userinfo included — the forging spelling's own hiding place), the URL owner, the request VO, and the device-flow session answering one refusal, and the space/tab adjudication pinned still-legal beside it. ocr28-7 (test:low): the manifest-unreadable row's apply() leaves dist/checksums.txt at 0000 and runState()'s finally restored only the other two chmod'd paths (rrmdir unlinks with no chmod fallback — the tree leaked per run on hosts whose unlink cannot remove a 0000 file); the census drove the owner choice (the forced-close leg restores its own twin at its own finally), and the restore lives at the row that broke it — the ocr27-9 doctrine — not in rrmdir, whose removal contract serves every caller.

RESIDUALS CARRIED FORWARD: NEW THIS ROUND — the SURROGATE VERSION-DRIFT note (above): the unescaper mirrors the DRIVEN engine (8.5.10, surrogates resolved); an engine generation that still refuses the class (the RFC-era behavior, lifted upstream) answers the eval-oracle pin RED naming the drift — a cross-version mirror (a PHP_VERSION_ID fence at the range check) is a decision-plus-fence for the day a host runs one, declined now as unbuildable on the unverifiable boundary (the exact version the upstream change landed in could not be established from the vendor record at round time; re-open with the UPGRADING note or a refusing engine in hand). The r27 head residual stands (the INSPECTOR twin's mid-path-link NO-TAIL spelling — wp_connectors_inspect_rrmdir('<symlink-to-dir>/sub') deleting the victim's subtree through the link, the r17-2 component-chain resolution living only in the harness twin); the ZAI-SUITE putenv census NOW FIVE ROUNDS OPEN; the rest of the r26 list stands (the sandboxed-'/' construction; the artifact-name/tearDown concurrency class; the crashed-run scratch residue; the scan_paths walk unfenced; the pid-suffixed scratch class; the ocr26-7 driven sim; the r21/r22 list); the order/runner flake did NOT reappear (the round's nine green full-check runs — three further invocations reddened deterministically at their own seams mid-round: the phpstan by-ref-capture errors at commit 1's first cut, the WordPress-reach token in a Url comment at commit 6's first cut — both fixed in-round, neither a flake). CLOSED THIS ROUND: the heredoc EOF boundary (the detector's twin lenses both flush at their last boundary now); the interrupted-absolute spelling (the name walk's absoluteness rides the token stream, not the byte string); the copyTree platform premise at the owner and its four ungated battery legs; the surrogate engine premise (driven, refuted, oracle-pinned); the line-derivation abort guard; the URL backslash adjudication (screened, one refusal through every consumer); the manifest chmod restore.

THE REWRITER'S FENCE FAMILY. ocr26-1 (bug:high, the round's head): wp_connectors_use_opens_import() draws its line from the FOLLOWER shape, and a name follower opens an import statement at the top level and a trait clause list inside a class body — the same bytes in both. THE ROUND'S REFUTATION OF RECORD — THE RDONLY 8.3 PREMISE (t31-ocr21-1, accepted bug:high; premise REFUTED by BOTH verifier lenses INDEPENDENTLY, corrected in-round as rd-1). The finding claimed ZipArchive::RDONLY was "registered only in the 8.3 cycle, never backported — php.net carries the 8.3.0 version chip" — an undefined-constant \Error on every 8.2 engine the composer floor admits. REFUTED AT THREE LEVELS, none requiring the other: (a) VENDOR — php.net's zip.constants.php carries the opposite chip ("Available as of PHP 7.4.3 and PECL zip 1.17.1, respectively, if built against libzip >= 1.0.0") and no 8.3.0 annotation anywhere on the page (the finding's cited chip is a misattribution — the web-search snippet both lenses tripped over names a neighboring constant); (b) SOURCE — the php-src PHP-8.2 stub registers RDONLY identically to 8.3 (under ZIP_RDONLY), PECL zip 1.17.1's release notes are the constant's actual birth, and PHP 8.2 bundles zip >= 1.21; (c) THE REPO'S OWN RECORD — the r19 ledger already read "available since 7.4.3, inside the floor" and DROVE the bare-constant reader on a real 8.2.33 engine to ER_NOENT, never \Error; a universal 8.2 fatal is incompatible with the round's own history two rounds back. WHAT SURVIVES: the BUILD corner the vendor record does name — a zip extension built against libzip < 1.0.0 (the extension's own build floor is 0.11) compiles no RDONLY, and the bare constant fatals exactly those engines, no PHP version boundary; the guarded spelling (defined(...) ? RDONLY : 0) stands as that corner's guard, verdict-identical on every engine that defines the constant (driven by the lens: flag 0 and RDONLY both open an existing archive, both ER_NOENT-no-create on a missing one), and the three pinned texts (reader docblock, pin docblock, pin failure message) state the true matrix now, with the re-open rule re-framed from "a PHP 8.2 engine" to "a zip extension built against libzip < 1.0.0". The ocr21-1 commit message stands as history, corrected here (the r19 precedent). The pin's no-escape claim survived STRENGTHENED: four escape shapes driven dead (namespaced class shadow leaves the global spelling defined, class_alias fatals on redeclare, disable_classes cannot touch extension classes, php -n keeps the compiled zip; runkit/uopz absent). THE ROUND'S LESSON, now doctrine: an engine-matrix premise is a VENDOR-RECORD claim — drive it at the source record (the stub, the constants page, the repo's own prior drives) BEFORE the fix lands; the finding's citation itself can be the fabrication. The phantom "8.3.0 chip" survived the driver's triage AND the fix commit because nobody fetched php.net until the verifier — one WebFetch at triage kills the class. (Contrast r19: that round's refutation was found in-round by the driver; r21's arrived one commit later, at the verifier, exactly because the finding's own citation went unchecked.)

THE TEST-FENCE EXEC CENSUS CLOSES (t31-ocr21-2, test:medium x3): the r20-named seven-site remainder gated — six tests (the three the finding's line refs named: the stage-tree pin with the ocr17-10 trio for both its proc_open spawns, the slug-rebuild and failing-rebuild twins on the exec pair, plus the three all-plugin/explicit-slug CLI legs the r20 remainder list carried at 2005/2024/2044), every guard before staging, driven: six visible skips under -d disable_functions=exec, the stage-tree pin one skip under proc_open. THE ROUND'S REFUTATION OF RECORD — THE RDONLY FLOOR PREMISE (t31-ocr19-1, accepted bug:medium; premise REFUTED, flag kept as pinned intent). REFUTED AT THREE LEVELS, all driven: (a) RUNTIME — a real docker php:8.2.33 engine (libzip 1.21.1; the verifier's own drive used 8.2.33/libzip 1.11.3, and the drive was INDEPENDENTLY RE-RUN for this ledger entry after the pass — same verdict both times): open($missing) with flags omitted returns int(9) ER_NOENT and file_exists() stays false, byte-identical observable behavior to the RDONLY spelling — no create on either side of the claimed engine boundary; (b) SOURCE RECORD — the php-src stubs declare `int $flags = 0` identically on PHP-8.2 and master, and the PHP-8.3 UPGRADING notes contain NO ZipArchive::open() default change (only the FL_RECOMPRESS deprecation, setArchiveFlag/getArchiveFlag, new constants) — the "8.3 engine change" the round's own first commit message cited does not exist; (c) CONSEQUENCE — the ocr10-5 strict gate (true === $opened) was therefore ALREADY LOUD on every engine in the support range, and the commit's "red at HEAD on the floor: empty list, no failure" never happened. WHAT SURVIVES: the explicit RDONLY stays as the read site's own statement of intent (a read never creates — the exact intent the new pin holds), and the missing-archive regression pin is real and driven-stable (RDONLY-open of a missing file deterministically returns ER_NOENT=9, never ER_OPEN, on both 8.2.33 and 8.5.10; the helper maps 9 to the named fragment). THE CENSUS RE-FRAMED: the thirteen same-shape omitted-flags sites the first commit listed (BuildSeamPropertyTest :595/:630; BuildArtifactsTest :382/:453/:1317/:2101/:2550/:2855/:3072/:4406/:6664; bin/inspect-artifact.php :43/:359) are STYLE VARIANCE, not latent exposure — there is no read-mode create-on-open on any engine in range. RE-OPEN RULE: only an ENGINE TRACE of a read-mode open() creating the archive (a real PHP/libzip build demonstrating it — none exists anywhere in this record), never re-derivation from the documentation legend. The two docblocks the first commit wrote in the false premise (the helper's and the pin's) were corrected in the docs commit; the first commit's own message stands as history, corrected here.

The ocr17-2 full-chain walk judged every component of the passed chain from '/' down, so on a host whose temp spelling itself crosses a system-layout link (macOS: TMPDIR under /var → private/var, the /tmp fallback → private/tmp) the FIRST link found was the host's own spelling — the probe named it for every temp-rooted path, rrmdir silently SKIPPED cleanup of every legal scratch tree, and copyTree refused every legal source. THE ROUND'S SUBSTANTIVE CLOSE — THE LANDING-POLICY HOLE, in two layers. Layer one (t31-ocr17-1, driven at HEAD on scratch): the ocr16-5 '/' sentinel judged the SPELLING'S chain, so a '..'-woven target whose ancestor walk anchors at an EXISTING component while the collapse resolves elsewhere escaped its loud refusal — copyTree($src, '/../<first-level>') anchored at the existing '/..', collapsed $target_real to a first-level nonexistent dir, passed every containment clause, and died in raw mkdir()/copy() warnings at the root's first level (permission-denied unprivileged, real first-level writes as uid 0) having RETURNED NORMALLY; the landing walk now judges the collapsed resolution by the sentinel's own rule, ROOT-ANCHORED regression leg, no byte lands. Layer two — the round's own verifier refutation, closed in-round as t31-ocr17-9 (the round-16 precedent, and the reason this ledger keeps the two-lens pass mandatory): the refutation lens DROVE a surviving counterexample through the fresh landing policy itself. The collapse was still LEXICAL, and the t31-ocr8-3 premise it rode ("the remainder holds no symlinks") holds only BELOW the anchor: a '..' in the remainder pops the collapse ABOVE the anchor it was resolved against, and the post-pop descent crosses symlink components nothing resolves. Driven (independently re-driven before the fix): copyTree($src, '<anchor>/b/../../link/dst') with link -> $src collapsed to '<parent>/link/dst' lexically, passed containment against the unresolved spelling, stopped the landing walk AT the link (is_link is a stop condition, never a resolution), and RETURNED NORMALLY with the copy landed INSIDE the source — the plain spelling of the identical landing refuses — while the file-link variant died in raw 'mkdir(): Not a directory' + copy() warnings (the ocr10-10 vocabulary-leak shape). The fix is a resolve-until-stable LOOP: the collapsed resolution walks the same judgment the spelling walked (sentinel, file crossing, dangling link, anchor realpath, collapse) and repeats until the judgment spelling IS its own resolution (a '/' resolution is stable — the root keeps the ocr9-9 mirror vocabulary, not the sentinel's); termination construction-evident (first collapse is dot-free, second pass's remainder is a nonexistent tail, third re-derives the same string). RE-OPEN RULE: only with a spelling whose PHYSICAL resolution escapes the loop — e.g. a normalizing-host extraction trace (the Windows-normalization class named in round 16's '..' entry) or a TOCTOU trace through the realpath-false fallback arm; the loop's unresolvable arm keeps the t31-ocr17-1 landing walk as its judge and belt.

THE SAFETY-NET DOCTRINE (t31-ocr17-3, the fixes file's own process note): destructive-spelling legs — the rrmdir '/'-family and the copyTree degenerate targets — pin their REFUSAL PRECONDITION before the destructive call (the spelling resolves where the guard says it does), because their safety otherwise rests entirely on the production refusal under test. THE DOCTRINE'S CEILING, ledgered from the verifier pass: a pre-call resolution pin CANNOT observe the guard (realpath never consults it), so the net covers spelling/platform drift only — guard regression is caught by the refusal assertions AFTER the (refused) call; routing the legs through an unprivileged subprocess would be the genuine belt (re-open only with that runner shape). Writing the pin drove its own ledgered discovery: realpath('') answers the CWD on this engine — a WORK DIR — which is exactly why the ocr11-22 guard judges the empty spelling lexically; the first draft of the pin rode realpath and reddened itself on it.

THE TWO-LENS VERIFIER PASS (a two-agent workflow, both lenses driven): the REFUTATION lens's surviving counterexample is layer two above (ocr17-9), and its second driven finding closed as t31-ocr17-10 — the exec-capability guard probed the established exec/escapeshellarg pair but this consumer SPAWNS through proc_open(), which the pair never consulted: under -d disable_functions=proc_open the guard passed and the first spawn fataled with the exact \Error the finding said must become a visible skip; the fix contract itself prescribed the pair, so the contract's prescription was insufficient, not just the implementation. The CONSISTENCY lens: gold-standard red runs of the NEW test files against a reconstructed pre-round harness (both production fixes re-reproduced through the committed tests, not just scratch drives), every census recounted (ocr17-6's 14 live sites exactly; ocr17-5's 5 sites), per-commit suite citations verified monotone, no-push verified. Zero other fix findings.

A braced block expires at its closing brace (global scope after — one semantics with PHP); a `use namespace Foo;` interrupted spelling lands in the ledger as a declaration (the shape predicate judges the keyword's follower, not the enclosing statement) — such bytes are a parse error PHP never accepts and the chain refuses them end-to-end regardless of which refusal fires; do not "fix" that entry away. - **The own-or-refuse-NAMED seam over legal import spellings, with its two carves (t31-ocr7-2 + t31-ocr7-7 + t31-ocr7-9)**: comma lists, close-tag termination, and comments inside the statement refuse with the class NAMED at the postcondition's throw path (green builds pay nothing; the postcondition stays the total authority); grouped `use …\{B, C};` stays OWNED (the member rewrite carries body commas at brace depth — do not re-survey it as unowned). The r7-2 saw_statement_brace carve stays as the php -l-illegal comma-then-group mix guard. The CASE-VARIANT axis refuses NAMED (keyword text, family-prefix spelling) — owning it (case-insensitive patterns) would flip the r7-pinned case-variant refuse doctrine (the battery's own leg); do not re-litigate that pin to "own" without a doctrine round.

The case-fold arm consumes the r6 deferred collision class's INSPECTOR half; the BUILDER-side fence over the collected entry set AND its directory prefixes (the file-vs-directory residual included) stays the r6 line's own round — do not flag a builder-shipped case-variant pair as new (it is the r6 deferred defect, now VISIBLE as a build-green/inspect-red split instead of silently green).
- **The embed-territory fold roles (t31-r12-10 + t31-r12-16)**: the writer's collision fence folds case; the inspector's exemption matches the CANONICAL prefix only. A case-variant spelling of src/Shared in a zip is FOREIGN (no builder-produced zip carries one — the fence refuses the whole tree), so its segments judge by the development-entry vocabulary. Do not re-unify the two folds "for consistency" — the unified fold was a REJECTED→ACCEPTED regression both lenses reproduced; do not narrow the writer's fence either.
- **The manifest regeneration doctrine (t31-r12-7, superseding the r4-8-era note)**: the merge PRUNES — an entry whose artifact no longer sits beside checksums.txt is dropped by the next successful run (a malformed line names no artifact and dies by the same rule), inside the merge lock. The pinned invariants: no run drops an entry whose artifact still exists beside the manifest, and no failure leaves a half-written file (a refused build lands no manifest change, prune included). Do not re-derive the old "record of what was built, not an inventory" posture.
- **The scanner prune is a dev-tree concept only (t31-r12-3, closing the r6 HIGH)**: wp_connectors_scan_paths($roots, $prune_dev_segments) — the repo scan keeps its traversal prune (a documented SUBSET of the development-entry vocabulary, not a second vocabulary); artifact scans pass false, always. The inspector's src/Shared dev-entry exemption exempts CLASSIFICATION, never content. Residual skips (the extension allowlist, the 2MB cap) are pre-existing and outside the prune claim — re-open only with a shipped-artifact shape they wave through that the round's claim covers.
- **The control-byte guard is ONE callable at the vocabulary owner (t31-r12-5)**: HeaderMap::assert_no_control_bytes() — the curated list, abort-as-reject, no new vocabulary. device_code, user_code, and PendingAuthorization's provider_id ride it; the authorization-code flow's code/state ride the flow payloads (PkceCodePair by RFC 7636 grammar, the device session by its own gates) — a future code/state field on PendingAuthorization joins the same guard, not a copy. Tab and space stay LEGAL in a code (they forge nothing; the header-value doctrine).
- **The bracket grammar whole (t31-r12-2 + t31-r12-18)**: an authority carries brackets only as ONE IP literal WRAPPING THE WHOLE HOST — exactly one '[' at the host's first byte, one ']', non-empty literal, and (the r11 legs) after the closer comes ':' or end with the port fully digits. The empty literal '[]' and mid-host pairs 'a[b]' refused; do not re-narrow to count-only.
- **The post-parse UTF-8 re-check is hardening, not a repro (t31-r12-8)**: the 8-bit-LC_CTYPE mangler is real C-library behavior (manufactured tr_TR.ISO-8859-9, ctype-proven live) but the engine folds are locale-independent since PHP 8.2 — the project floor (the strtolower-ascii RFC). The pin manufactures the locale through localedef into a private LOCPATH and restores LOCPATH BEFORE the locale with a checked restore: glibc resolves the restore THROUGH LOCPATH, and a partial locale dir makes restoring the original name FAIL silently — the leak broke later tests' `/i` matching (PCRE caseless consults the active locale on this runtime) roughly one random-order run in three before the round's own verifier pass caught it. Do not setlocale in a test without this restore shape.
- **The CLI-entry idiom (t31-r12-11 + t31-r12-17; ALL FIVE converted by t31-ocr3-8)**: wp_connectors_cli_entry() is the one guard + diagnostics spelling (build, check-conventions, lint-php, inspect-artifact, and — since OCR round 3 — scan-secrets, which was the last script wearing the r9-4-class file-top diagnostics); it reads the AUTO-GLOBAL $argv — $_SERVER['argv'] is unpopulated under a variables_order ini without "S" (GPC), where every entry script was a silent exit-0 no-op (reproduced). A new bin/ entry script takes the helper, never a hand-rolled copy.
- **The scoped composed-tree scan (t31-r12-12)**: wp_connectors_self_containment_violations($pluginDir, $scanRoot) narrows the WALK, never the ANCHOR — build's staged postcondition walks the embed destination subtree only (the pre-gate judged the plugin files byte-for-byte against the same anchor; ~6 fewer tokenized files per example-connector build), and an include anchored at the plugin root above the subtree stays LEGAL (mutation-proven: a narrowed anchor refuses it). Do not pass the subtree as $pluginDir.
- **The printable seam (t31-r12-15/r12-19; premise corrected t31-r13-1 under the re-open rule)**: wp_connectors_printable() neutralizes every C0 control and DEL in archive-controlled text interpolated into inspector diagnostics. PREMISE CORRECTED UNDER EMPIRICAL REPRO (r13): this runtime's ZipArchive sanitizes control bytes in entry names on NEITHER side — addFromString() keeps a newline-bearing name and getNameIndex() returns it byte-exact (both probed again in r13; the r12 "sanitizes both sides" probe was wrong), so the verdict lines interpolating entry names were a LIVE forged-line defect (reproduced), not hardening — six sites fixed as t31-r13-1 and the merged helper lines as t31-r13-4. The ENGINE-DIAGNOSTIC arm (the captured extractTo() warning) still carries no raw C0 byte on this build: libzip's renderer substitutes the bytes with visible glyphs (U+25D9 for a newline, U+2190 for ESC — probed byte-level). The ZipArchive WRITER also refuses byte-exact duplicate names (the fence's regression rides a raw stored-zip builder, kept in BuildArtifactsTest).
- **Deferred with fix shapes**: the builder-side case-fold entry fence over the final collected set AND its directory prefixes (the r6 line above, inspector half consumed); the parameterization of buildPlugin's shared-source root if a second caller with a different layout ever appears (t31-r12-14's docblock states the coupling and the wrong-content failure mode); the `>2MB`/extension-allowlist artifact-scan residuals (pre-existing, no producer in the fixtures).
- **Refuted (t31-r12 lenses)**: "extractTo() throws ValueError on an unusable destination" — on this runtime it WARNS and returns false (captured, then refused by the extraction check); the exception-safe capture restore (t31-r12-20) is kept as defense-in-depth against the engine's ValueError migration, pinned structurally. Also refuted as a defect claim: the trait dedup's var_export/serialize output — byte-identical before and after (the r11-5 ledgered doctrine).

## M3 / Task 3.1 fix round t31-r11 (branch feat/m3-task31-contracts, 2026-09-15)

Fix round over round-11's eight counted findings (two correctness with exit-0 repros — the namespace-relative use statement that shipped a parse error through the real builder, and the hidepid liveness misread that contradicted the sweep's own charter; the serialization-channel redaction hole; three smaller — the dotted-slug test's permanent dist/ pollution, the locale-dependent identifier folds, the CONVENTIONS doc drift; two tool-output — the chr() deprecation mid-gate), fixed as t31-r11-1..8 per the driver's adjudication on #1 (the rewriter OWNS the relative spelling; the "refuse all relatives" path explicitly rejected). A two-lens verifier pass (independent correctness + security agents over the whole round diff; every raised finding adversarially re-derived by an independent verifier) raised SIX findings — three distinct defects after the cross-lens duplicates (both lenses independently found the relative-member splice and the interrupted-relative ride) — ALL CONFIRMED with reproduced evidence (two exit-0 ships through the real builder, both produced by the round's own new code path), ZERO-of-one refuted (the var_export/serialize bypass), all fixed in-round as t31-r11-9..11. Suite 1572 → 1581 tests, 44418 → 44539 assertions, 2 skipped unchanged. Do not re-derive:

- **The relative-use doctrine (t31-r11-1 + t31-r11-9 + t31-r11-10)**: a `namespace\`-relative in a USE statement is a parse error on every runtime (8.5.10 verified) — it never "adapts through the rewrite" (the r8-2 premise is false in the use position; CODE positions keep the carve-out, where the premise holds: the relative resolves against the file's own rewritten declaration). The rewriter owns the spelling in BOTH lexings — the fused T_NAME_RELATIVE token AND the interrupted keyword (trivia between `namespace` and the name drops the fused token; inside an open use statement the bare keyword is never legal PHP, so every T_NAMESPACE met there is owned): resolved against the declaration IN EFFECT (multi-block correct), family-rewritten, spliced as the fully-qualified import (legal only in STANDALONE use forms — plain, aliased, function, const). Refusals: no declaration in effect, escaping resolution, sibling resolution, the group-PREFIX shape, and any relative inside a group BODY (the FQ member the splice would emit is an illegal spelling — r11-9's exit-0 ship). The detector owns the spelling in use positions (a survivor on rewritten bytes is a rewriter miss); the sweep is the STRICTER verdict on source bytes by design (the spelling is not one legal PHP accepts; lint owns the source side). Do not propose refusing all relatives (adjudicated against), composing anything inside group braces, or restoring the use-position carve-out.
- **An invisible /proc entry is not a death verdict (t31-r11-2)**: hidepid=2 makes another user's LIVE build invisible in /proc; liveness concludes DEAD only on a deterministic answer (visible entry, EPERM, or ESRCH past the posix probe) and stays ALIVE when nothing can tell — the sweep's own charter. The /proc entry probe is injectable for the regression. Do not propose "invisible ⇒ dead" shortcuts or /proc-only fast paths that bypass the signal probe.
- **The bracket-authority grammar rule (t31-r11-3 + t31-r11-11)**: after the last `]` comes `:` or the end of the authority — the allow form, abort-refusing (glm36-8). parse_url() misreads EVERY glued spelling identically (host '[:', port 1 — 159 visible-ASCII spellings swept), not just digits; the empty port after the colon still belongs to the r4-12 digit check. Do not re-narrow the ban to digits or propose normalizing glued spellings.
- **The debug-dump channel vocabulary (t31-r11-5)**: every secret-carrying VO's __debugInfo() mirrors the masked forms — HeaderMap is the ONE header-render owner (private rendered_value() shared by rendered_lines(), masked_headers(), and its own dump), SecretMask::mask() for token/verifier/device-code/user-code positions, redacted URL + omitted body for the HTTP VOs, nested-set passthrough for StoredGrant (the engine applies the payload's dump at every level — PendingAuthorization needs no mask of its own). The user code masks CONSERVATIVELY (it is the pairing capability, not display text). var_export() and serialize() bypass __debugInfo() BY ENGINE DESIGN — they are the export/persistence channels, never debug rendering; the docblocks enumerate the covered set (print_r/var_dump/debugger views) and debug_zval_dump honors it (verified). Do not flag the bypass as a contract hole, and never feed these VOs to error_log(var_export(…)).
- **The slug→identifier folds are ASCII-only (t31-r11-6)**: bin/lib's wp_connectors_ascii_lower/upper() byte tables are the tooling-side twins of shared AsciiFold (standalone file, no autoloader — shared doctrine, not a shared class). The r2-14 BY-SCOPE doctrine covers comparison-key folds (consistent under any locale); DERIVED IDENTIFIERS do not get that license — they are keyed vocabulary spelled bare by plugin files. No tr_* locale exists on the development host (locale -a: C, C.utf8, en_US.utf8, POSIX — the regression attempts setlocale and restores in a finally; the divergence is argued from the fold tables, AsciiFold's own posture). Do not route bin/lib through the shared class or re-widen to locale folds.
- **Forwarded, not acted on (round-11 triage)**: OAuthRateLimitException rejecting negative Retry-After vs RefreshPolicy clamping — one semantics for the 3.3/3.7 round to own, forwarded alongside r1-15; lint-php.php's stdin vacuous-exit-0 (a spelling caveat, not a defect — no stdin caller exists); five cleanup items below the 15-cap join the r5 backlog lines (duplicated scratch helpers, the bootstrap-prefix literal, the unpinned acronym table, the fixture version-constant drift, the pid-less `.checksums-*` orphans).
- **Refuted (t31-r11 verifier)**: the "serialization channel rides the redaction contract" var_export/serialize claim — engine physics real (var_export ignores __debugInfo; serialize is the by-design storage channel), defect claim false: every __debugInfo() docblock opens by enumerating the covered functions, no shipped code calls var_export/error_log on these VOs, and the round strictly reduced exposure (byte-identical var_export output before and after). The exclusion sentence lives HERE rather than in the docblocks: var_export()/serialize() are export/persistence, not debug rendering.

## M3 / Task 3.1 fix round t31-r10 (branch feat/m3-task31-contracts, 2026-09-15)

Fix round over round-10's eight counted findings (two correctness with end-to-end exit-0 repros — the trait-adaptation laundering that UPGRADED the r8-noted misparse debt to a demonstrated ships-broken channel, and the b/B-prefixed literal that blinded the value lens; three smaller — lint-php's unguarded file scope, the stage-tree race the r5-11 ledger entry itself names, the headers() doc-drift; three cleanup — the hand-maintained provider pattern, the seen_lowercase parallel, the second slug→identifier derivation), fixed as t31-r10-1..8. A two-lens verifier pass (independent correctness + security agents over the whole round diff; every raised finding adversarially re-derived by a third agent) raised TEN findings — five distinct defects after the cross-lens duplicates (both lenses independently found the symlink deletion and the SPEC-pin fail-open) — ALL CONFIRMED with reproduced evidence (one an exit-0 ship through the real builder; the three walk shapes A/B'd as PRE-EXISTING at the pre-round base, completeness debt of the same machinery rather than regressions), ZERO refuted, all fixed in-round as t31-r10-9..13. Suite 1569 → 1572 tests, 44283 → 44418 assertions, 2 skipped unchanged. Do not re-derive:

- **The brace-machinery classification vocabulary (t31-r10-1 + t31-r10-9 + t31-r10-11), consummating the r8-noted adaptation debt**: the walk's use-statement taxonomy is closed over FIVE rules — (1) a brace behind a NAMESPACE SEPARATOR is the GROUP-USE prefix (`use Prefix\{…}`: prefix unreported, members reported COMPOSED); (2) a brace glued DIRECTLY to a clause opens a TRAIT-ADAPTATION block (`use SomeTrait {…}`: the glued clause and every member report as un-composed 'code' positions, the `as` skip never arms); (3) a MULTI-TRAIT list (`use A, B {…}`) reports its pre-brace clauses as un-composed 'use' import positions (the flag arms at the brace — the classification is the intent, not a bug: a family clause there refuses at both gates through the rewrite-ownership verdict, never laundered); (4) an ABSOLUTE run (leading backslash) NEVER composes against a group prefix and is never alias-skip material; (5) the alias skip eats only BARE identifier runs, and a group statement that reports no member reports its PREFIX (the empty-body fence). Do not propose composing anything inside braces, re-arming the skip for qualified spellings, or "fixing" the multi-trait clause kind — each was a laundering channel or a verdict-drift vector the round closed.
- **The b/B prefix is value-free (t31-r10-2)**: a b-prefixed literal computes exactly what its unprefixed twin computes; the value lens strips the prefix before taking the quote and both enclosures normalize identically. Do not special-case the prefix in the text lens (the raw bytes are judged there, as ever).
- **The scratch-sweep doctrine (t31-r10-4 + t31-r10-10 + t31-r10-13), consuming the r5-11 reopen ("then the stage dir wants the PID too")**: stage trees are PID-named; the startup sweep reclaims (a) `.stage-<slug>-<pid>` DIRECTORIES and (b) `.connectors-<slug>-….zip.tmp-<pid>…` FILES (sidecar `.sha256` and libzip's in-window `.<rand>.part` included) whose process is DEAD — /proc liveness, signal-0 posix fallback (EPERM = alive), never-delete when undeterminable. NEVER touched: live-pid scratch, any SYMLINK (is_dir follows links; rrmdir through a stage-shaped link deleted the TARGET tree — a link is never this code's product and stands where it is), the pid-less `.stage-<slug>` pre-r10 spelling, and the pid-less `.checksums-*` manifest staging temps (unattributable — reclaiming one could race a LIVE run's staging). A link at the run's OWN stage name refuses the build loudly. A dead pid reused by an unrelated process keeps its orphan until that process dies (conservative direction). Do not propose sweeping foreign-shaped scratch or racing pid-less temps.
- **Every bin/ entry file carries the CLI guard (t31-r10-3, closing the r9-4 class repo-wide)**: build.php, inspect-artifact.php, check-conventions.php, scan-secrets.php, and now lint-php.php keep diagnostics, walks, and exits inside `realpath($argv[0]) === __FILE__` — the file scope carries only requires. Do not re-flag file-scope ini flips or walk-on-require in any of them; a new bin/ script takes the same guard shape.
- **The provider-neutrality vocabulary rides the SPEC (t31-r10-6 + t31-r10-12)**: PROVIDER_SET (provider ID => vendor aliases) mirrors SPEC §1's connector table, the banned pattern is DERIVED from it (preg_quoted, word-bounded, case-insensitive), a SPEC-sync pin fails loudly in BOTH directions (a provider without a set row, a retired one left behind), and a ROW FENCE requires every digit-first table row to parse into an ID (a deviant row shape fails, never skips). Adding a provider = SPEC row + set row, nothing else. Do not re-derive the vocabulary from connector directories (planned providers must be banned BEFORE their connector exists) and do not propose slug-segment derivation ('oauth' would ban the shared tree's own subject matter); the whole-ID words (zai_anthropic, claude_pro) are deliberate vocabulary.
- **The VO headers() caveat is BYTE-IDENTICAL in both files (t31-r10-5)**: the all-digit-name PHP-canonical integer-key behavior is stated in both value objects' docblocks with the HeaderMap owner's own vocabulary — do not reword one side, and do not "fix" the int keys themselves (unavoidable PHP array semantics, invisible to lookup/render, pinned by execution).
- **The duplicate fence probes the one folded index (t31-r10-7)**: the seen_lowercase parallel is deleted; isset() on the canonical digit-string key coerces to the same int slot the store lands in, so the all-digit fence is the same fence by construction. Do not reintroduce a parallel key structure.
- **One slug→identifier core (t31-r10-8)**: wp_connectors_identifier_from_slug() (over wp_connectors_slug_segments()) owns the separator vocabulary, the acronym casing, and the digit-initial underscore for BOTH spellings — the namespace suffix (camel glue) and the version-constant stem (underscore glue) — with byte parity against the former hand spellings pinned at cutover. Do not hand-spell either derivation beside the core (the twins were synchronized by hand twice before it existed).

## M3 / Task 3.1 fix round t31-r9 (branch feat/m3-task31-contracts, 2026-09-15)

Fix round over round-9's nine counted findings (the headliner: the r8-5 ALM arm encoded the WRONG bytes — U+065C \xD9\x9C, a visible Arabic vowel sign — while the real U+061C \xD8\x9C passed), one publish-path one-verdict drift, one 3.2-envelope gap, six smaller items, plus the round's noted item (clock-port identity wording) fixed as the one-line shape. Suite 1560 → 1569 tests, 44212 → 44283 assertions, 2 skipped unchanged. Do not re-derive:

- **The bidi vocabulary is a CURATED BYTE LIST of invisible direction/joining material, never a Unicode-category derivation (t31-r9-1 + t31-r9-10)**: the r9-1 lesson cuts both ways — a category claim in prose ("only format controls are banned") drifted from the bytes exactly the way the r8-5 byte arm drifted from its own documentation. Visible content — letters, the Mn vowel signs, U+065C included — stays LEGAL obs-text (the r1-19 hospitality; the counter-pin renders an Arabic letter + vowel sign verbatim). Invisible material OUTSIDE the list is the r2-7 spelling-curated posture, a curation DECISION and not a miss: variation selectors (Mn, glyph-choosing), the word-joiner/invisible-math run U+2060-U+2064, the deprecated direction-PROCESSING controls U+206A-U+206F, U+180E, tag characters (U+E0001, U+E0020-U+E007F), interlinear annotation marks (U+FFF9-U+FFFB), U+1BCA0-3, U+1D173-17A — do not re-flag these as coverage gaps without a producer; do not propose a category-derived ban (the exhaustive check lives in the r9-10 commit: zero bidi-ACTIVE Cf pass).
- **The build's composed-tree scan runs at the STAGING path, embed-only (t31-r9-2)**: wp_connectors_self_containment_violations() over $stage/<slug> after the embed loop composes, never over shared/src in place — the anchored-include escape verdicts are depth-identical either way (src/Shared maps one-to-one), but the staged scan is literally the tree the zip packs, so build and inspect judge one artifact shape. Without embed_shared the staged tree is a copy of the pre-gated plugin files (the non-PHP LICENSE excepted) and the scan is skipped — not a hole, an idempotence guard. Do not propose a valid-UTF-8 screen instead (weaker than the BCP grammar) and do not add exceptions for "token-shaped" non-ASCII (no producer; the envelope's JSON-encodability is the pinned reason).
- **The storage boundary reconstructs; identity is not a contract (t31-r9-5)**: the in-memory fake round-trips through serialize/unserialize at BOTH save and load, the port's load() docblock disclaims instance identity, and the port tests pin by value (accessors + to_array() as the compare) with assertNotSame in both directions. - **The stream-wrapper scratch rule (t31-r9-8)**: a one-segment stream URL ('scheme://name') has a scheme-ONLY dirname ('scheme:') that PHP treats as a plain relative path — any seam calling @mkdir(dirname($to)) creates a literal directory of that name in the CWD. Test stream URLs are two segments deep and the wrapper owns its namespace's mkdir. Re-open only if another seam starts deriving paths from stream URLs.

Fix round over round-8's seven counted findings — three state-machine/predicate gaps in the r7 token detector (each with an exit-0 repro: the close-tag statement boundary the r7-7 reset missed, the T_NAME_RELATIVE resolution gap, the text lens's source/target-only vocabulary), two HeaderMap surface defects (the LRM/RLM/ALM direction marks below the bidi class's byte range; the missing UTF-8 gate at rendered_lines), the directory-casing axis of the r5-3 class (the fence lives in the ONE collector the embed and sweep both ride), and the double-tokenization cleanup. Suite 1554 → 1560 tests, 44107 → 44212 assertions, 2 skipped unchanged. Do not re-derive:

- **The relative-operator ADAPTATION carve-out (t31-r8-2's doctrine)**: a `namespace\…` spelling resolves against whatever its file declares, so under a rewrite-owned tree (the source root, or the target root when judging rewritten bytes) it ADAPTS through the rewrite in any position and must report nothing — a blanket report-when-family would refuse the pinned legal operator (`namespace\FormsFixture` under an own-root declaration, riding verbatim through the rewrite). Only a family-resolving relative under a base the rewrite does not own reports, under its own 'relative' kind — never 'use', whose rewritable-position reading would wave the un-rewritable spelling through the sweep while the build's postcondition refuses it. Do not propose either half's opposite.
- **The sibling exclusion owns the dedicated patterns' FULL below-vendor tails (t31-r8-9)**: 'Shared' and '<Suffix>\Shared' — never the suffix segment alone. The suffix-segment cut waved target-SEGMENT siblings (`…\<Suffix>\OAuth`) through the build postcondition while the sweep refused the same file — the r7-8 verdict-drift class one segment inside the target tree. The exclusion exists for diagnostics (the dedicated patterns run first and return); its separator tolerates one-or-two backslashes so a raw-view stem match cannot preempt a double-backslash spelling's fuller unescaped-view report. Do not widen it.
- **The embedded tree's fence: one declaration, case-exact directories, depth included (t31-r8-4 + t31-r8-8)**: the collector refuses a second namespace block outright (the embed stages ONE path per file), a missing declaration, an outside-root declaration, and any below-root casing/depth disagreement with the staged path. The ROOT's own casing is deliberately not the fence's charge (the family detector and the rewrite postcondition own it). The BASENAME axis is NOT fenced: `helper.php` declaring `class Helper` collects and ships dead exactly like the directory axis did — fix shape: parse the type declaration and compare its stem to the basename (a new walk product; the namespace walk does not collect type names). Re-open only to build that fence.
- **The render-vocabulary decision (t31-r8-6)**: invalid-UTF-8 bytes in a header value render PERCENT-ENCODED at the render seam, never rejected at construction (obs-text is legal per t31-r1-19 and the pinned obs-text rendering stays verbatim) and never replaced with U+FFFD (encoded keeps the value diagnosable). r4-13's URL surface rejects because the URL constructor owes no obs-text hospitality; the header render seam owes the same OUTCOME (no invalid UTF-8 ever leaves, json_encode never drops the line) by encoding. Do not propose rejecting at the header constructor or masking with a replacement character.
- **The rewriter owns only ';'-terminated declarations and uses (documented in the r8-1 test)**: `namespace X ?>` / `use … ?>` are legal PHP statements the rewrite patterns (anchored on ';') do not rewrite — the postcondition refuses them loudly. Extending the rewriter to tag-terminated spellings is a rewriter round, not a detector fix; do not flag the refusal as a detector false positive.
- **One line semantics for both lenses (t31-r8-11)**: the engine's token lines and the text lens's \R-class count agree on \n, \r\n, and lone \r — every terminator a PHP file carries. The \R class also matches \v/\f/\x85/…, where the engine does not: a divergence there needs a vertical tab or form feed between a token start and a finding — exotic beyond any real source; re-open only with a producer.
- **Trait-adaptation block misparse (round-8 noted item, detector-correctness debt)**: `use T { … insteadof … }` inside a class body parses to zero family carriers and the loud-refusal direction is the stated t31-r3-2 contract — but the walk MISPARSES the adaptation block (mangled composed names, the brace-depth state machine was built for group-use). Fix shape: recognize the adaptation-block shape in the walk and report its names as code positions; a future round.
- **WP-reach vocabularies live only in the PHPUnit sweep (round-8 noted item, r2-7 dev-gate design)**: bin/build.php does not re-run the WP-reach gates over shared/src — the sweep's curation pins (import vocabulary, WP-reach) are dev-time only. A release-channel re-run is a design decision (which vocabularies gate a SHIP, not just a dev tree); ledgered for a future round, do not flag as a build hole.
- **Valueless `--slug` / unchecked chmod-touch (round-8 noted items, consciously unfixed)**: `php bin/build.php --slug=` (empty string) is falsey to the CLI's isset and falls through to the all-plugins mode; normalize()/touch() return values are unchecked (chmod 0644/touch with FIXED_MTIME on a staging tree the build owns — a failure surfaces at the checked write/rename seams behind them). Both observed, both accepted this round; re-open only with a shape where the unchecked call is the LAST word on bytes that ship.

## M3 / Task 3.1 fix round t31-r7 (branch feat/m3-task31-contracts, 2026-09-14)

Fix round over round-7's five counted findings — all in the build/rewrite seam, and all one more spelling of the SAME class the seam had carried since t31-r4: the K1 survivor surface (regex-scanned) was "one spelling away" for the THIRD time (a comment between a use name's segments defeats every contiguous probe including the r4-K1 survivor pattern; a sibling import under the vendor prefix ships pointing at a namespace no plugin tree loads; a double-backslash class-string's bytes never spell the single-backslash name; the sweep's every-use-statement whitelist never asked whether the rewrite actually rewrites; plus the unchecked generated-member write). THE TERMINAL DECISION, recorded with the spelling history that motivated it — r2's case-variant posture (consciously accepted), r3-2's per-spelling probe, r4 K1's total regex, r6's case/junk folds, r7's four confirmed one-more-spellings: **the regex-patch strategy on this seam is DONE; namespace-reference detection is TOKEN-level now.** ONE detector (wp_connectors_shared_family_references(), bin/lib/plugin-tools.php, the one implementation the build's postcondition and the sweep's namespace gate both ride) reassembles name runs across comments and whitespace (a comment can only INTERRUPT a run, never carry one — the interrupted spelling dies by construction), composes group-use members with their prefix, judges string literals by their UNESCAPED runtime value (quoted, heredoc, nowdoc — octal/hex/unicode escapes included), judges comment/docblock and inline-HTML text with the former survivor pattern (now generated from any family spelling, byte-identical for the source side), and reports PCRE aborts as findings (glm36-8). The remaining counted finding (t31-r7-3) fixed the generated-member write seam: writeNormalized() owns file_put_contents()'s return (false or short → loud refusal naming file + expected bytes) and re-verifies the staged bytes on disk — the t31-r5-S "verified whole at its staging path" claim now holds for generated members too.

A two-lens verifier pass (independent correctness + security agents over the whole round diff; every raised finding adversarially re-derived by a third agent before fixing, all four CONFIRMED with end-to-end repros) held the round's fix-direction and falsified the round's own implementation claims three times — the follow-ups fixed as t31-r7-6/7/8: the run assembly dropped the SEPARATOR byte on trivia-after-separator joins (the whitespace spellings the r4 regex refused had become exit-0 ships — a genuine regression, confirmed twice by both lenses independently); the alias skip survived its use statement's end and silently ate the next name run anywhere in the file; and the postcondition waved target-rooted CODE/STRING references through (a hand-spelled dangling reference to the building plugin's own prefix shipped at exit 0 while the sweep refused the same file — verdict drift), with the text lens blind to target spellings entirely. All three pinned red-under-pre-fix in both batteries. Suite 1552 → 1554 tests, 43763 → 44107 assertions, 2 skipped unchanged. Do not re-derive:

- **The SIBLING doctrine (doctrine flip, superseding r4-era pins)**: in shared/src and in rewritten outputs, the only legal family references are the own tree (Deicod\WpConnectors\Shared…) and — post-rewrite, and ONLY in declaration/use positions (t31-r7-8's measured allow-set) — the target prefix Deicod\WpConnectors\<Suffix>\Shared. The r4-era pins that 'Deicod\WpConnectors\SharedStorage\Widget' and the '{Other\Shared as O}' group member stay untouched-legal are SUPERSEDED (they were regex-discrimination pins, not dependency doctrine); the bare vendor-prefix import ('use Deicod\WpConnectors;') refuses too. Do not flag these refusals as false positives — the rewriter owns no sibling spelling and a plugin's private tree loads nothing else.
- **The K1 split-composed boundary, HALVED (superseding the r4 boundary's static half)**: the double-backslash literal — and every STATIC escape spelling (octal, hex, \u{...}) — is judged by its unescaped VALUE now and refuses. The RUNTIME half stands unchanged: interpolated string chunks and concatenated pieces are judged by their TEXT only (byte parity with K1); their values exist only at runtime. Re-open only if a shared source ever resolves classes dynamically — the fix shape remains a no-dynamic-class-resolution gate, never a wider pattern.
- **The unparseable-input posture**: PHP 8 refuses whitespace/comment-interrupted names and double-backslash names in CODE (php -l verified for every position tried), so those spellings cannot appear in a lintable file — but the build's postcondition judges rewritten bytes BEFORE any lint gate and the sweep judges independently of it, so the detector handles interrupted runs anyway. The totality claim is the walk's own docblock; do not propose dropping the reassembly on lint grounds.
- **The sweep's dry-run layer IS the narrowed whitelist (t31-r7-5's fix shape)**: a use statement is sweep-legal exactly when the rewrite DID rewrite it — verified by rewriting each swept file through the build's own postcondition (suffix ExampleConnector), so the sweep and the build give ONE verdict by construction. Do not propose a re-implemented ownership vocabulary in the sweep; the dry run owns it.
- **The verifier follow-ups' honest residuals**: the group-prefix peek only fires for a statement's FIRST name (a nested brace group's inner names compose with the OUTER prefix — unparseable PHP judged anyway, fail-loud); a heredoc containing an interpolation piece is judged by its TEXT chunks but not its value (the runtime half above); the stream-wrapper short-write pin drives PHP's own write loop (file_put_contents folds a short total into false — "Only X of Y bytes written" — the checked write owns BOTH spellings, so no short-count path exists to pin separately).
